  _Anubhav Jain, Takashi Shibuya, Yuya Kobayashi, Yuhta Takida, Nasir Memon, Julian Togelius, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Text-to-image diffusion models have rapidly transitioned from research prototypes to widely deployed tools. Along with their impressive capabilities, these models can also generate harmful or undesirable content (e.g., NSFW imagery), motivating practical mechanisms for removing such concepts. Negative prompting, which uses the negative-prompt prediction as the guidance anchor, is among the most widely used mechanisms for steering sampling trajectories away from unwanted concepts. However, for jailbreak prompts, it can fail in a regime we refer to as \emph{guidance collapse}, where the model's denoising predictions for the prompt and negative prompt become similar, so that the guidance difference driving negative prompting vanishes and the guided prediction degenerates to the negative-prompt prediction, undermining suppression. To fix this issue, we propose TraSCE (Trajectory Steering for ConcEpt Suppression), a training-free inference-time method that makes negative prompting reliable under guidance collapse. TraSCE combines two complementary components: (i) unconditional anchoring, which makes the fallback under guidance collapse a neutral unconditional prediction rather than the negative-prompt prediction, and (ii) an alignment-localized steering step, which deliberately induces this collapse for adversarial prompts and steers the sample to the unconditional fallback, while remaining minimally intrusive on benign prompts. We evaluate TraSCE on adversarial prompt benchmarks targeting nudity and violence, and further demonstrate its applicability to suppressing artistic styles and objects. Across these settings, TraSCE substantially improves the robustness of negative prompting to jailbreak prompts without any training, weight updates, or pre-collected concept-specific data (prompts or images), while keeping the impact on general image quality small.
  </details>

- **[Paint Outside the Box: Synthesizing and Selecting Training Data for Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2412.00684)**  `arXiv:2412.00684`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.LG`  
  _Zilin Du, Haoxin Li, Jianfei Yu, Boyang Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual grounding aims to localize the image regions based on a textual query. Given the difficulty of large-scale data curation, we investigate how to effectively learn visual grounding under data-scarce settings in this paper. To address the data scarcity, we propose a novel framework, POBF (Paint Outside the Box and Filter). POBF synthesizes images by inpainting outside the box, tackling a label misalignment issue encountered in previous works. Furthermore, POBF leverages an innovative filtering scheme to select the most effective training data. This scheme combines a hardness score and an overfitting score, balanced by a penalty term. Extensive experiments across four benchmark datasets demonstrate that POBF consistently improves performance, achieving an average gain of 5.83\% over the real-data-only method and outperforming leading baselines by 2.29\%-3.85\% in accuracy. Additionally, we validate the robustness and generalizability of POBF across various generative models, training data sizes, and model architectures.
  </details>

- **[PruneGround: Plug-and-play Spatial Pruning for 3D Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2606.31148)**  `arXiv:2606.31148`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI` `cs.CL`  
  _Duc Cao Dinh, Khai Le-Duc, Florent Draye, Chris Ngo, Terry Jingchen Zhang, Bernhard Schölkopf, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D Visual Grounding (3DVG) aims to localize target objects in 3D scenes given natural language descriptions. Existing approaches typically perform reasoning over the entire scene, leading to ambiguous predictions and high computational cost, especially in cluttered environments. We observe that many referential expressions rely on local spatial context and often correspond to restricted spatial regions rather than the full scene. Motivated by this insight, we propose PruneGround, an effective plug-and-play framework for 3DVG built upon three key components. First, we introduce Language-Guided Spatial Pruning (LGSP), which leverages a frozen Vision Language Model (VLM) to identify language-relevant regions, thereby reducing spatial computation and grounding candidates in the narrower search space. Second, we propose MultiView-Conditioned Description Reformulation (MCDR), which decomposes complex expressions into simplified target--anchor relations and augments missing spatial cues through multi-view reasoning. Finally, we propose LLM-Grounder, which repurposes a detection-pretrained spatial LLM into a language-conditioned grounding model by aligning point cloud and linguistic representations within the pruned region. Extensive experiments on the three most popular point cloud benchmarks demonstrate that our method achieves state-of-the-art results on all three ScanRefer settings and on 9 out of 10 Nr3D/Sr3D settings. Code and models are publicly available:this https URL
  </details>

- **[COMET: Decoupled Distillation, Routing, and Capacity Control for Task-Agnostic Continual Vision--Language Learning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22907)**  `arXiv:2610.22907`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Seungyoon Woo, Keonhee Park, Gunhee Kim_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-language models (VLMs) such as CLIP retain zero-shot recognition ability, but their open-world use often requires task-agnostic continual learning: tasks arrive sequentially, past data are unavailable, task identity is unknown, and the backbone cannot be retrained. We argue that this setting fails VLMs through three coupled pressures beyond the stability-plasticity tradeoff: (1) modal-consistency drift, (2) representation-space interference, and (3) per-task capacity shortfall. We propose COMET (Collaborative Mixture-of-Expert Transfer) to resolve these three issues respectively as follows. A frozen teacher provides only a feature reference on a shared image-text pool; Tri-Affinity Distillation preserves image-image, text-text, and image-text geometry; and Student-Aware Cross-Expert Distillation aligns new experts with compatible prior experts so routing errors degrade gracefully. We design COMET to keep capacity and inference separate: a contextual bandit keeps or merges full per-task experts using post-training signals, while a reconstruction router selects sparse experts at test time without teacher semantics. On the X-TAIL benchmark, we show that each proposed component improves continual learning performance. COMET shows that continual vision-language adaptation can preserve multimodal geometry, coordinate accumulated experts, and allocate capacity jointly when feature reference, expert shaping, routing, and capacity control are separated by construction.
  </details>

- **[HAPACT: A Benchmark For Human-Centric Physical Impact Localization in Movies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22895)**  `arXiv:2610.22895`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Youngrae Kim, Yejin Jang, Eunho Kim, Youjin Sung, Kun-Woo Song, Sangpil Kim, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Physical reasoning about humans in videos has largely focused on everyday interactions, where contact, pressure, pose, and motion support embodied perception and contact-aware applications. Still, short and forceful properties like impact events remain relatively underexplored, despite their relevance to broader understanding of how abrupt physical events affect the human body. To fill this gap, we introduce human-centric impact localization, a new task that localizes target physical impacts temporally within a video and spatially on the human body. We further present HAPACT, a HumAn-centric imPACT dataset and benchmark built from movie clips, containing over 2.8K video-query pairs with more than 20K fine-grained human-annotated frames. We built HAPACT around viewer-grounded annotation, text-conditioned localization, and supervision at frame-level temporal and SMPL-X-based spatial granularity. To demonstrate the effectiveness of our benchmark, we conduct extensive evaluations of existing methods, employing video moment retrieval models for temporal localization and human-scene contact models for spatial localization. For the spatial task, we propose a text-conditioned baseline by reformulating localization as query-guided body-region selection. Benchmark results show that physical impact localization remains challenging for existing methods, motivating HAPACT as a dedicated benchmark to support physical impact reasoning in videos.
  </details>

- **[STRIDE: Evaluating Spatiotemporal Reasoning in Driving Edge Cases](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22795)**  `arXiv:2610.22795`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Lieqi Liu, Rui gao, Jia-Chen Gu, Wenbo Hu, Zhaobin Mo, Ahmadreza Moradipari, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  In autonomous driving, perceiving surrounding objects is not enough: failing to understand how they move and interact over time can lead to dangerous decisions. Evaluating vision-language models (VLMs) therefore requires testing whether they connect past spatial observations to future consequences. Yet existing benchmarks often assess edge cases through basic perception questions or test temporal understanding in simple scenarios, leaving complex spatiotemporal reasoning underexplored. We introduce STRIDE (SpatioTemporal Reasoning In Driving Edge Cases), an object-centric benchmark for long-tail, safety-critical driving scenarios. STRIDE covers six task families: spatial perception, spatial understanding, temporal memory, temporal extrapolation, trajectory prediction, and scene-context awareness. It pairs multiple-choice questions probing individual spatiotemporal judgments with related free-form visual question answering (VQA) requiring detailed scene explanations and justifications of driving decisions. We prioritize scenes with unusual object behaviors and complex traffic interactions, generate reference answers from structured dataset annotations through automated procedures, and use limited language-model assistance and human verification to ensure annotation quality. Across 12 proprietary, open-weight, and driving-specialized models, even the best performer, GPT-6-Astra, achieves only 43.4% average multiple-choice accuracy, versus an approximately 20% random-choice baseline. Further analysis reveals that strong performance in describing driving scenes does not necessarily translate to accurate safety-critical judgments, and that models struggle more with reconstructing object motion than recalling past positions. STRIDE thus exposes spatiotemporal driving limitations that aggregate VQA scores can conceal.
  </details>

- **[VideoHarness-SRSI: Structured Recursive Self-Improvement for Video Understanding Harness](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.19424)**  `arXiv:2610.19424`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Zihao Lin, Haoyu Dong, Haobo Xu, Chi-Chih Chang, Xuying Ning, Yinglong Xia, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  LLM system capabilities depend not only on model parameters but also on the executable harnesses that organize information acquisition, tool use, reasoning, and memory. Recursive harness evolution can automate their design, but how it can reliably discover generalizable systems from limited, noisy, and repeatedly reused feedback remains unclear. We study this question in agentic video understanding, where rich multimodal evidence and coupled mechanisms make harness optimization challenging. We first introduce VHarness-BRSI, a recursive self-improvement framework that evolves video harnesses around a frozen vision-language model. Analyzing over 4,000 evolved candidates, we find it competitive with hand-engineered systems but identify three interconnected failure modes: validation overfitting, search-budget dilution, and the early-experience trap. We trace all three to a common root: the same imperfect validation evidence drives both harness revisions and the proposer's search strategy. We therefore propose VHarness-SRSI, an evidence-governed framework in which every search decision rests on evidence that is transferable, sufficient, and gathered before the search commits. It restricts the proposer to content-free feedback, informs exploration with instrumented warm-up experiments, and develops mechanisms in isolated probe-race branches before factorial composition and joint refinement. Across four video understanding benchmarks, VideoHarness-SRSI improves the equally weighted mean held-out score by 6.2% relative to VideoHarness-BRSI and reduces the mean validation-test gap by 37.9%. Reliable harness evolution thus depends on how search is organized and informed, not only on how well it optimizes validation performance.
  </details>

- **[TORINO: Token Reduction via Interpretable Concept Overlap in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.04593)**  `arXiv:2607.04593`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Riccardo Renzulli, Gabriele Spadaro, Shruthi Gowda, Alaa Eddine Mazouz, Van-Tam Nguyen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Vision-Language Models (VLMs) have demonstrated impressive capabilities across different tasks, but their computational cost is dominated by the large number of visual tokens fed to the language model. Existing token reduction methods rely on attention-based scores or pairwise similarity, without an explicit semantic representation of each token. We introduce TORINO (TOken Reduction via Interpretable coNcept Overlap), a plug-and-play framework for adaptive visual token reduction in VLMs that requires no fine-tuning of the underlying model. TORINO leverages Sparse Autoencoders (SAEs) to project visual tokens into an interpretable latent space where token relationships can be analyzed through shared concept activations. Specifically, we define concept overlap as the degree of agreement between active SAE latents and use it to group tokens that share semantic content. Reduction within each group is then performed by either pruning or merging, providing a unified framework that preserves semantically important visual information while removing redundancy. Unlike fixed-budget approaches, TORINO dynamically adapts the reduction rate to input complexity, allowing different images to retain different numbers of tokens. Experiments across multiple vision-language benchmarks show that TORINO achieves favorable efficiency-accuracy trade-offs, reducing the number of visual tokens with minimal performance loss. Source code released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Scalable Adaptation of 3D Geometric Foundation Models via Weak Supervision from Internet Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.07891)**  `arXiv:2602.07891`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Zihui Gao, Ke Liu, Donny Y. Chen, Duochao Shi, Guosheng Lin, Jia-Wang Bian, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Geometric foundation models show promise in 3D reconstruction. However, their progress is severely constrained by the scarcity of diverse, large-scale 3D annotations. While static-scene Internet videos are abundant and diverse, utilizing them as a scaling source for geometric learning is challenging due to the absence of ground-truth geometry and the presence of observational noise. To address this, we propose SAGE, a framework for Scalable Adaptation of GEometric foundation models from raw video streams. SAGE leverages a hierarchical mining pipeline to transform videos into training trajectories and hybrid supervisions: (1) Informative training trajectory selection; (2) Sparse Geometric Anchoring via SfM point clouds for global structural guidance; and (3) Dense Differentiable Consistency via 3D Gaussian rendering for multi-view constraints. Besides, we also introduce a regularization strategy that replays a small fraction of supervised data to prevent catastrophic forgetting. Applied to two architecturally distinct backbones, SAGE reduces Chamfer Distance on unseen benchmarks (7Scenes, TUM-RGBD, Matterport3D) by 20-42% with MV-DUSt3R and consistently improves VGGT. SAGE establishes Internet video as a viable and scalable adaptation source for geometric foundation models. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[AQUA-Net: Adaptive Frequency Fusion and Illumination Aware Network for Underwater Image Enhancement](https://arxiv.org/abs/2512.05960)**  `arXiv:2512.05960`  `cs.CV` `cs.AI`  
  _Munsif Ali, Najmul Hassan, Lucia Ventura, Davide Di Bari, Simonepietro Canese, Jungpil Shin_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Underwater images often suffer from severe color distortion, low contrast, and a hazy appearance due to wavelength-dependent light absorption and scattering. Simultaneously, existing deep learning models exhibit high computational complexity, which limits their practical deployment for real-time underwater applications. To address these challenges, this paper presents a novel underwater image enhancement model, called Adaptive Frequency Fusion and Illumination Aware Network (AQUA-Net). It integrates a residual encoder-decoder with dual auxiliary branches, which operate in the frequency and illumination domains. The frequency fusion encoder enriches spatial representations with frequency cues from the Fourier domain and preserves fine textures and structural details. Inspired by Retinex, the illumination-aware decoder performs adaptive exposure correction through a learned illumination map that separates reflectance from lighting effects. This joint spatial, frequency, and illumination design enables the model to restore color balance, visual contrast, and perceptual realism under diverse underwater conditions. Additionally, we present a high-resolution, real-world underwater video-derived dataset from the Mediterranean Sea, which captures challenging deep-sea conditions with realistic visual degradations to enable robust evaluation and development of deep learning models. Extensive experiments on multiple benchmark datasets show that AQUA-Net performs on par with SOTA in both qualitative and quantitative evaluations while using less number of parameters. Ablation studies further confirm that the frequency and illumination branches provide complementary contributions that improve visibility and color representation. Overall, the proposed model shows strong generalization capability and robustness, and it provides an effective solution for real-world underwater imaging applications.
  </details>

- **[Learning to Correct Geometry in Generated Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23856)**  `arXiv:2610.23856`  `cs.CV`  
  _Weijie Lyu, Tiancheng Shen, Jason Li, Yujing Wang, Xiaoyuan Wang, Haobo Yuan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Generated videos often lose geometric consistency under camera motion: structures warp under viewpoint change, parallax becomes inconsistent, and smooth camera movement may collapse into shot-like transitions. We argue that this failure is largely a data problem. In web-scale video corpora, most visible motion comes from dynamic objects captured by static or weakly moving cameras, leaving only sparse supervision for viewpoint-induced appearance changes. To address this, we propose Video Geometry Corrector (VGC), a post-hoc video-to-video model trained on synthetic paired supervision. Starting from real videos with diverse camera motion and pose annotations, we construct VG-Pairs with a synthetic paired-supervision data generation pipeline. Each video clip is treated as a geometry-consistent target and paired with a distorted counterpart synthesized by a pretrained image-to-video inpainting model using trajectory-adaptive keyframes. VGC learns to map the distorted input to the geometry-consistent target without modifying the source generator, and uses optical flow as an auxiliary cue to help disentangle camera motion from object motion. Experiments on pose-conditioned and prompt-driven benchmarks show that VGC improves geometric consistency across multiple source generators, and scaling to a larger backbone further extends the framework toward more general video refinement.
  </details>

- **[The Collection of a Real-World, Large-Scale Dataset for Automated Skill Evaluation in Central Line Dressing Change](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23789)**  `arXiv:2610.23789`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ummey Tanin, Shannan K. Hamlin, Hsin-Mei Chen, Peizhu, Qian_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Central line-associated bloodstream infections (CLABSIs) remain a major source of preventable morbidity and mortality in healthcare settings. Proper execution of the central line dressing change (CLDC) procedure is essential for infection prevention, yet current approaches to competency assessment rely primarily on manual ob- servation by clinical educators, making evaluation time-intensive, difficult to scale, and limited in consistency and granularity. These challenges highlight the need for AI-assisted skill evaluation methods that can support more objective, reproducible, and scalable assessment of clinical performance. However, progress in this area has been constrained by the lack of broad-scale, clinically grounded datasets for developing and evaluating such methods. In this work, we present the collection process of a large-scale video dataset for CLDC skill evaluation, comprising 365 recordings of registered nurses collected during the 2026 annual competency exam at Houston Methodist Hospital. Each video is paired with subtask-level temporal annotations and clinical performance assessments spanning 17 subtasks of the procedure. We describe the data collection and curation pipeline, including pilot testing of record- ing configurations, annotation protocol design, and post-collection processing. We further establish the feasibility of video-based skill assessment through benchmark experiments with spatiotemporal deep learning models. This dataset provides a foundation for future research on automated clinical skill assessment, feedback generation, and AI-assisted training in nursing education.
  </details>

- **[Video Diffusion Avatars: Taming Video Diffusion Models into Controllable 3D Avatars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23772)**  `arXiv:2610.23772`  `cs.CV`  
  _Simon Giebenhain, Tobias Kirschstein, Liam Schoneveld, Davide Davoli, Zhe Chen, Matthias Nießner_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video diffusion models (VDMs) trained on internet-scale data encode a rich prior on appearance, motion, and physics that is difficult to replicate with explicit 3D rendering and costly 3D data capture. We introduce Video Diffusion Avatars (VDAs), a method that creates photo-realistic, multi-view consistent avatars from a single image by fine-tuning a VDM for precise control over camera viewpoint, facial expression, and upper-body pose. Departing from 3D morphable models (3DMMs), VDAs use a learned expression space that lifts the expressiveness ceiling of 3DMMs, e.g., for the tongue and mouth interior. However, learned expression codes exhibit heavy viewpoint bias, as our DISTRACT benchmark reveals. We show that this bias can be removed through disentanglement, enabling their use as driving signals for multi-view consistent avatars. Camera and upper-body pose are controlled jointly via proxy-mesh renderings, giving independent control over expression, pose, and viewpoint. A camera-triplet training strategy makes the approach efficient: our models train for 34 hours on a single A100 using only public multi-view data. VDAs outperform state-of-the-art single-image 3D avatars on novel-view reenactment, reducing face LPIPS by 29% and improving perceptual quality (JOD) by 66% on BecomingLit. A static 3DGS reconstruction probe further confirms that our generations are the most multi-view consistent. Built on a video prior, VDAs produce effects beyond the reach of explicit renderers, including physically plausible hair dynamics, shadows, reflections, and refractions.
  </details>

- **[MTEdit: Packed Trajectories Beat Rollouts for Multi-Turn Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23751)**  `arXiv:2610.23751`  `cs.CV`  
  _Houyuan Chen, Fanzhou Wang, Xiangyu Fan, Songlin Yang, Zhitao Yang, Lvmin Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-turn image editing aims to modify an image according to a sequence of instructions, making it better aligned with human-AI collaborative creation than single-turn editing. However, constructing high-quality multi-turn training data remains challenging, as conventional sequential rollout accumulates errors and introduces visual-quality drift across editing turns. To address this challenge, we introduce MTEdit, a multi-turn image editing dataset built through a pipeline centered on packed trajectory canvas (PTC) generation. By jointly generating multiple editing states within a unified visual context, this pipeline reduces visual-quality drift across turns. Covering both step-wise and context-aware editing scenarios, MTEdit comprises 500K multi-turn image editing trajectories, with up to 8 turns and an average of 5.38 turns per trajectory, making it one of the most extensive datasets for multi-turn image editing. We further introduce MTEdit-Bench, a benchmark designed to evaluate both scenarios by assessing models' ability to follow editing instructions, maintain content consistency, resist visual-quality drift, and effectively use historical context across turns. Using MTEdit, we fine-tune Qwen-Image-Edit-Causal to obtain Qwen-Image-MTEdit. On MTEdit-Bench, we evaluate a broad range of closed-source and open-source image editing models, including Qwen-Image-MTEdit. Experimental results show that Qwen-Image-MTEdit achieves the best performance among the evaluated open-source models, demonstrating the effectiveness of MTEdit in improving models' multi-turn image editing capabilities.
  </details>

- **[FedRAPP: Federated Test-Time Prompt Tuning for Vision-Language Models with Reliability-Aware Prompt Personalization](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23705)**  `arXiv:2610.23705`  `cs.CV`  
  _Syed Md. Ahnaf Hasan, Md Akil Raihan Iftee, Sajib Mistry, Amin Ahsan Ali, AKM Mahbubur Rahman_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Federated prompt learning enables vision-language models like CLIP to collaboratively adapt lightweight soft prompts across distributed clients without sharing raw data. However, existing federated prompt learning methods are designed primarily for supervised settings with labeled client data and assumed relatively stable training distributions. At test time, source-free federated prompt tuning remains largely unexplored. Clients may receive unlabeled target samples with heterogeneous, often non-IID distributions that differ substantially across clients and from the source training distribution, causing locally adapted prompts to drift in different directions and reducing the effectiveness of federated aggregation. In this work, we explore Source-Free Federated Test-Time Prompt Tuning (SF-FTTPT), where distributed clients collaboratively adapt soft prompts on unlabeled test streams without access to source data. Our study reveals that conventional federated aggregation methods struggle under heterogeneous client distributions, as locally adapted prompts become increasingly specialized and difficult to combine effectively. To address this challenge, we propose FedRAPP, a personalized aggregation framework that combines reliability-aware client assessment, server-side exponential moving-average prompt memory, and similarity-guided peer aggregation. FedRAPP selectively incorporates information from reliable and compatible clients while preserving local specialization, enabling more robust adaptation under distribution shift. Extensive experiments across nine CLIP benchmark datasets and ImageNet variants demonstrate that FedRAPP consistently outperforms standard federated aggregation baselines under heterogeneous client distributions while maintaining the efficiency advantages of prompt-only adaptation.
  </details>

- **[PCDFusion: Proposal-Context-Detail Bayesian Rendering for Infrared-Visible Image Fusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23663)**  `arXiv:2610.23663`  `cs.CV`  
  _Rui Liu, Lin Gu, Hesong Li, Ying Fu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Infrared-visible image fusion (IVIF) aims to combine visible and thermal infrared observations for human perception and downstream vision tasks. Visible and thermal infrared observations are physically complementary but unreliable in different ways. Existing deep IVIF methods mainly improve feature interaction and reconstruction, but often lack an explicit local rule for deciding when visible appearance should dominate and when thermal responses should contribute. To address this issue, we reformulate IVIF as local Bayesian posterior rendering, where fusion is controlled by posterior action weights rather than direct modality mixing. In this paper, we propose Proposal-Context-Detail Fusion (PCDFusion), a visible-anchored framework that implements this formulation through three rendering stages. PCDFusion preserves potentially useful thermal responses, updates exposure-degraded visible regions, and renders reliable band-limited infrared structures into the final luminance. Extensive experiments on public IVIF benchmarks show that PCDFusion improves fusion quality, target recovery, and downstream detection and segmentation performance under challenging illumination. The code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[FlexPart: Text-Controlled Semantics-Aware 3D Part Segmentation at Flexible Granularity](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23659)**  `arXiv:2610.23659`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yiqun Zhao, Jiachen Xu, Yihao Zhi, Yunfei Zhao, Zibo Zhao, Xintong Han, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  A 3D asset admits multiple valid part decompositions, while semantics-aware segmentation should reflect both the desired granularity and the parts a user wants to extract. Ideally, a practical segmentationmodel should strive for a simple principle: what you ask for is what you get. However, realizing this goal is hindered by two core obstacles: the lack of multi-granularity training data and the absence of aholistic control mechanism. We propose FLEXPART, a unified framework for text-controlled 3D mesh segmentation. To establish flexible control, we introduce a holistic control prompt comprising semantic partnames with location and shape clauses. To support this mechanism, we utilize an automated Vision-Language Model (VLM) pipeline to construct a large-scale dataset of multi-granularity partitions and theircorresponding prompts. Driven by this data, FLEXPART generates a view-independent part-identity field in native 3D space, directly assigning discrete part IDs to mesh faces. To compel the model to respectsemantic conditions rather than rely on geometric priors, we train the model using randomized background dropping. Furthermore, an inference-time VLM planner can automatically complete descriptive clausesfrom 2D renderings, allowing users to simply name the desired parts in ordinary words. FLEXPART with planner-completed prompts outperforms the strongest evaluated baseline by 15% in mIoU, even when thatbaseline receives ground-truth guidance. On our held-out FLEXPARTBENCH, FLEXPART leads at each granularity and improves average mIoU by 22% over the strongest baseline. With only the requested part namessupplied by the user, it also improves IoU by 35% over the strongest click-driven baseline on selective segmentation.
  </details>

- **[PanoBench: A Panoptic Occupancy Prediction Benchmark for Autonomous Driving](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23537)**  `arXiv:2610.23537`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yi Feng, Zizhan Guo, Junwu E, Yu Ma, Mengtan Zhang, Jin Wu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Panoptic occupancy unifies 3D geometry, semantics, and instance identities, but its development is limited by scarce instance-aware annotations. Existing benchmarks mainly provide semantic labels reconstructed from incomplete sensor observations, while panoptic datasets have limited domain coverage or annotation completeness. We introduce PanoBench, a camera-centric panoptic occupancy prediction benchmark spanning synthetic and real-world driving scenes. It combines PanoBench-CarlaOcc, PanoBench-nuScenes, and PanoBench-KITTI360 through canonical data interfaces and unified evaluation protocols, providing multi-resolution annotations with persistent instance identities. The synthetic branch derives complete panoptic geometry from simulated meshes. For real-world data, a new pipeline reconstructs backgrounds using geometric priors and topology constraints, generates foreground mesh assets from multi-frame multi-view images, and fuses them into sparse panoptic voxels. A unified valid-mask protocol evaluates complete thing instances supported by temporal observations while scoring stuff regions at the voxel level. Analyses of observation fidelity, geometric completeness, spatial continuity, and temporal consistency demonstrate the benefits of the resulting annotations over existing occupancy benchmarks. We benchmark representative methods on semantic occupancy prediction, panoptic occupancy prediction, and 4D occupancy forecasting, and study voxel resolution, synthetic-to-real transfer, and storage efficiency. These results reveal complementary strengths of volumetric and ray-based metrics and persistent challenges in real-world instance-aware prediction. PanoBench is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[PreciseEdit: Strong Yet Stable Semantic Transport for One-Step Image Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23535)**  `arXiv:2610.23535`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jingyi Wang, Xiao Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent one-step text-to-image (T2I) models enable efficient image synthesis, opening up new possibilities for interactive Web-based image editing. However, existing one-step editing methods often construct a global transport field through simple vector operations between conditional predictions. While such strong transport can effectively drive the image toward the target semantics, it may be released along unreliable directions or over non-edited regions, leading to structural distortions within the editing region and background drift elsewhere. More importantly, stabilizing the editing process through global smoothing or uniformly weakening the transport field often makes large semantic changes overly conservative. In this work, we revisit one-step image editing from the perspective of the tension between semantic strength and transport stability, and identify a key insight: strong semantic transport is not inherently unstable. What matters is whether it is applied along reliable directions and at reliable spatial locations. Motivated by this insight, we introduce PreciseEdit, a model-agnostic, training-free and inversion-free one-step editing framework that strengthens target-semantic alignment while preserving background fidelity and structural consistency. We further propose a dual-noise-scale role decomposition strategy: a higher-noise scale produces the strong target-directed transport used for actual editing, whereas a lower-noise scale jointly serves as a probe for directional agreement and spatial structure. Extensive experiments on PIE-Bench demonstrate that PreciseEdit achieves state-of-the-art performance and effectiveness on various editing tasks.
  </details>

- **[ReVision: Reprogramming Image Generators for Dense Visual Perception](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23533)**  `arXiv:2610.23533`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yitong Yang, Yinglin Wang, Xuexin Liu, Wei Chen, Zhaoyang Xu, Jiaxin Lin, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent image generators exhibit rich visual priors that can support dense visual perception. However, existing approaches typically encode multiple perception tasks into a shared parameterization, making joint training sensitive to the mixture and weighting of heterogeneous data and prone to cross-task interference. To address this issue, we introduce ReVision, an instruction-conditioned parameter generation framework built on a frozen multimodal understanding model and a frozen image generator. Given an image and a free-form instruction, the understanding model jointly encodes them into a multimodal image--instruction representation, which a trainable parameter generator maps to transient low-rank updates for the generator. Each image--instruction pair induces a distinct set of transient prediction parameters, enabling instance-specific adaptation while both pretrained backbones remain unchanged. ReVision requires no task-specific heads, explicit task identifiers, or stored adapters. A shared RGB output interface unifies segmentation, depth estimation, and surface-normal prediction. Across diverse dense prediction benchmarks, ReVision achieves competitive performance with state-of-the-art methods while supporting flexible task specification through natural-language instructions. Our analysis further reveals substantial gradient disagreement across tasks, highlighting the conflict introduced when heterogeneous objectives share a single parameterization. Meanwhile, the generated updates exhibit structured task- and input-dependent organization in weight space, further validating instance-level parameter generation for multi-task perception.
  </details>

- **[ShadowCast: On-the-Fly Context-Aware Shadow Augmentation for Remote Sensing Image Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23440)**  `arXiv:2610.23440`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yechan Kim, Dongho Yoon, Jongmin Joo, JongHyun Park, Moongu Jeon_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Shadows cast by elevated objects alter the spectral appearance and obscure the boundaries of objects in remote sensing images, thereby degrading semantic segmentation performance. Most existing approaches handle shadows as degradations to be detected and removed, requiring additional costs from dedicated networks and shadow-specific annotations. We propose ShadowCast, which takes the opposite approach: it synthesizes shadows and injects them during training for remote sensing segmentation. It extracts masks of elevated objects, such as buildings and trees, directly from the segmentation labels and estimates an image-specific cast-shadow direction cue by probing the brightness around them. It then projects synthetic shadows along the inferred cast-shadow direction, requiring no learned network and no acquisition metadata. Experiments on three widely used remote sensing benchmarks, Vaihingen, Potsdam, and LoveDA, show that ShadowCast consistently outperforms the baseline across diverse state-of-the-art segmentation architectures. Moreover, it brings additional performance gains when combined with standard augmentation and regularization techniques, indicating that stochastic shadow injection serves as a simple and complementary augmentation strategy for remote sensing segmentation.
  </details>

- **[GeoReason: Bridging Logical Reasoning and Spatial Fidelity in Remote Sensing Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23416)**  `arXiv:2610.23416`  `cs.CV`  
  _Cheng Fan, Han Wu, Tingzhang Luo, Minjing Dong, Yehui Tang, Jianyuan Guo_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have significantly advanced instruction-driven perception through the "Embedding-as-Mask" paradigm. However, extending this capability to Remote Sensing (RS) reasoning segmentation remains exceptionally challenging under cluttered geographical contexts and extreme scale variations, often leading to Spatial Fidelity Degradation and Target Misidentification. We identify a critical yet underexplored bottleneck: the sequential alienation of the segmentation token [SEG] from its initial visual grounding within the autoregressive generation paradigm. Produced only at the end of long reasoning chains, [SEG] relies on visual evidence that has been progressively diluted by linguistic abstraction and autoregressive noise. This information decay is particularly catastrophic for RS targets with minuscule pixel footprints, where even marginal fidelity loss leads to localization failure. To address these bottlenecks, we propose GeoReason, a novel framework designed to fortify logical deduction and preserve spatial integrity. First, we introduce an Anticipatory Prior mechanism that injects the [SEG] token directly into the initial user query, shifting it from a terminal output to a primary condition. This paradigm shift transforms the token into a persistent spatial anchor, preventing information decay during long-form linguistic inference. Second, we enhance visual reasoning via a Location-aware Chain-of-Thought (CoT) that enforces coarse-to-fine localization using bounding boxes, coupled with a Latent Patch-to-Token Alignment (PTA) Loss that explicitly aligns the latent segmentation token with physical RS textures. Extensive experiments demonstrate that GeoReason consistently outperforms state-of-the-art methods on the Earthreason and RRSIS-D benchmarks. Code is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Can We Use Saliency Maps for the Evaluation of Generative Image Models?](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23411)**  `arXiv:2610.23411`  `cs.CV`  
  _Eleonora Ristori, Luca Bindini, Marco Lippi, Paolo Frasconi_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Evaluating generative image models remains challenging, as commonly used metrics depend on fixed pretrained feature extractors optimized for different tasks that do not always align with human realism perception across datasets and model families. To address this limitation, we investigate a human-inspired evaluation framework that avoids representations learned for unrelated tasks. Specifically, we train a generator-specific classifier to distinguish real images from samples produced by a given model and analyze its decisions using saliency maps. Across diverse benchmarks, we observe that these classifiers learn generator-specific artifacts rather than a universal notion of "fakeness", exhibiting strong accuracy and limited cross-generator transfer. This makes saliency analysis particularly informative: the resulting attribution maps highlight visual cues and structural irregularities characteristic of each model's outputs. By combining classification accuracy and saliency information, we obtain a composite score aligned with human judgments, without relying on large pretrained backbones and using only real and generated samples from the evaluated model.
  </details>

- **[FloodVLM-Pix: Language-Guided Pixel-Level Flood Understanding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23377)**  `arXiv:2610.23377`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chengyang Zhang, Zhihao Wang, Zhiyu Long, Xueqian Wang, Quanming Yao, Wei Wu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As climate change intensifies, floods are becoming more frequent and destructive, making accurate flood understanding an urgent priority. However, existing vision-language models perform poorly on flood-related tasks, suffering from attentional bias and inaccurate descriptions of flood magnitude. To bridge this gap, we construct the first large-scale remote sensing flood multi-modal benchmark that supports language-level flood understanding and pixel-level flood segmentation. The benchmark contains 131,200 flood image patches and corresponding 443,100 textual descriptions for flood severity and evolution. Based on this benchmark, we propose FloodVLM-Pix, a language-guided pixel-level flood understanding model that includes a flood prior module and a multi-temporal flood pixel decoder to achieve accurate flood understanding. The flood prior module automatically distills sub-region flood spatial priors and inputs priors into FloodVLM-Pix, solving the attentional bias problem in flood language understanding. The flood pixel decoder introduces a novel general-specific feature fusion mechanism to address performance degradation caused by interference (e.g., clouds or mountain shadows) during flood segmentation. Experiments on FloodVL-Pix demonstrate that FloodVLM-Pix improves both flood description quality and pixel-level flood segmentation performance compared with state-of-the-art VLMs and flood segmentation models. Our code and data will be released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[MOSS-Image Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23374)**  `arXiv:2610.23374`  `cs.CV`  
  _MOSI.AI_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Recent closed-source image generation systems can produce diverse visual content from simple compositions to information-dense graphics, yet open-weight text-to-image models still struggle with dense text and intricate layouts. To narrow this gap, we introduce MOSS-Image, an open generation system that combines high-fidelity visual synthesis with intent-aware composition. First, we pretrain a 5B single-stream diffusion transformer (DiT) from scratch with a carefully designed recipe, enabling bilingual long-form text rendering and precise layout control. Second, we develop an intention-aware agentic prompt enhancement (PE) module that translates diverse user requests into detailed content and layout specifications. Together, they enable MOSS-Image to generate text-rich and layout-coherent images. We further introduce LongLongTextBench (LLTB) for evaluating long-form English and Chinese text rendering, and RealLayoutArena (RLA) for end-to-end layout-rich generation. MOSS-Image achieves state-of-the-art performance among open-weight models on LLTB, with an average text coverage of 96.3%. On RLA, MOSS-Image ranks second in the end-to-end system comparison, while its backbone ranks first among generators evaluated with shared enhanced prompts. We will release the model, training recipe, and evaluation framework to support further research and promote the text-to-image community.
  </details>

- **[CatchUp: Depth-Aware Re-Admission of Visual Tokens for Efficient Large Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23324)**  `arXiv:2610.23324`  `cs.CV`  
  _Eunju Park, Uiwon Hwang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Large Vision-Language Models (LVLMs) repeatedly process hundreds of visual tokens across decoder layers, incurring substantial computation and memory costs. Existing inference-time visual token pruning methods often irreversibly remove low-ranked tokens, although the set of visual tokens receiving high query-conditioned attention can change across decoder depth. Recoverable routing preserves skipped tokens for later reconsideration, but re-admits them with stale representations after bypassing intermediate transformations. We further find that replacing stale re-admitted states with their current-depth oracle states from a full-token reference trajectory substantially improves grounding performance. We therefore propose CatchUp, a training-free framework for depth-aware visual-token re-admission. CatchUp repeatedly re-evaluates query-conditioned visual relevance, processes only selected visual tokens through each decoder interval, and preserves deferred tokens for later re-admission. Upon re-admission, CatchUp approximates missed representation evolution using reference-to-current transition signals from semantically and spatially related visual tokens. Across three referring-expression grounding datasets and six multimodal benchmarks, CatchUp achieves the highest average relative retention in both benchmark groups at 64-, 128-, and 192-token budgets. At a 64-token budget with 88.9% fewer active visual tokens, CatchUp retains 92.17% of the full model's performance on average across six multimodal benchmarks while reducing end-to-end latency by 8.0%. Code is available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Xiaomi-Lens-0 Technical Report](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23279)**  `arXiv:2610.23279`  `cs.CV`  
  _Pei Fu, Yike Liu, Yue Sun, Hanmo Wang, Xiuwen Xi, Ruoceng Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) answer questions about whole images well, yet they still struggle with fine-grained region understanding. A key reason is that text-only supervision never specifies which regions support an answer. To close this gap, we present Xiaomi-Lens-0, a 4B MLLM built on Qwen3.5 that predicts masks from its own visual representations. Specifically, a lightweight decoder combines vision-encoder patch features with visual latents, the language model's mask-token states, so mask supervision reaches the shared backbone without an additional segmentation encoder. Because each latent decodes to a mask, the model can interleave reasoning with region-specific latents and thus think with visual latents, while grounded reasoning traces supervise the regions it visits during chain-of-thought. As a result, across all eight RefCOCO, RefCOCO+, and RefCOCOg splits, Xiaomi-Lens-0 gains 5.6 points on average over Qwen3.5 in referring comprehension and achieves the highest segmentation cIoU among compared models. Moreover, it surpasses Qwen3.5 across all perception benchmarks. Homepage: \href{this https URL}{this https URL}
  </details>

- **[Agentic Image Generation in the Wild](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23269)**  `arXiv:2610.23269`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yifan Li, Bowen Qin, Kun Zhou, Haochen Zhao, Zikang Liu, Zheng Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Agentic image generation pairs an image generator with a search agent that retrieves references from the web, extending generation beyond the generator's training data. Real-world requests, however, often concern entities that appeared only days ago. We call this setting Image Generation in the Wild. In this setting, the web offers limited visual evidence, search results are crowded with easily confused entities, and the target is rarely shown on its own. To study this setting, we build an automated pipeline that synthesizes prompts from recent headlines and keeps only those whose real search results are highly confusing, yielding requests that are hard to solve yet natural to ask. With it, we construct Savanna, a benchmark of 500 prompts across 16 subcategories. On Savanna, even harness frameworks built on the frontier models retrieve a correct reference for only about half of the prompts. We then propose Locate-Corroborate-Isolate, a paradigm in which the agent first pins down the target's identity through text search, then verifies candidate images against multiple sources, and finally crops a clean reference for the generator. Trained on 15K trajectories that follow this paradigm, Ranger-8B achieves a search score of 67.2, outperforming harnesses equipped with frontier models. It also achieves the best generation quality with every generator we test, and surpasses prior agentic methods on other image generation benchmarks. We release the pipeline, benchmark, training data and model atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[LonGaussian: Tracking Foundation Priors for Long-Context Feedforward Gaussian Reconstruction](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23250)**  `arXiv:2610.23250`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhoufeng Xie, Guanjun Wu, Shuai Zhang, Xinggang Wang, Wenyu Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstructing high-quality, renderable 3D scenes from long videos is essential for robotic perception and embodied intelligence. Many feed-forward Gaussian reconstruction methods rapidly predict 3D scenes from a small set of images, but extending them to longer sequences presents two bottlenecks: jointly processing all views increases GPU memory requirements, while accumulating pixel-aligned predictions redundantly represents the same surfaces and increases storage and rendering costs. We present LonGaussian, a streaming feed-forward framework that reconstructs compact, globally renderable Gaussian scenes from long image sequences with known intrinsics and unknown camera poses. Built on a pretrained streaming 3D reconstruction backbone, LonGaussian decodes long-context geometric features into dense Gaussians. Within each chunk, multi-frame point tracks link repeated observations of the same surface and guide a learned merger to consolidate the corresponding Gaussian predictions. Rendered-depth consistency filtering further suppresses Gaussians from other chunks that incorrectly occlude locally reconstructed surfaces. On DL3DV and RealEstate10K benchmarks, LonGaussian improves novel-view rendering quality while using substantially fewer Gaussians than the evaluated feed-forward baselines.
  </details>

- **[RAP: A Dataset for Paper-to-Poster Generation via Declarative Markup from Human-Crafted Exemplars](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.23230)**  `arXiv:2610.23230`  `cs.CV`  
  _Haonan Han, Yukang Lin, Minliang Lin, Haoxian Tan, Jun-Yan He, Rui Yang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Making a conference poster is visual coding in miniature: an entire paper must be compressed onto a single page, which exercises summarization, visual design, and code implementation at once, using nothing beyond the paper itself. Yet benchmarks for this task in the context of visual coding remain scarce: existing resources are small and annotated only with layout boxes, so evaluation must fall back on VLM-as-judge or human checklists rather than objective render-and-compare, and the generation methods they support design layouts from scratch, leaving human-crafted posters unusable as either a retrievable design library or as verified supervision. We introduce RAP (Reconstruct A Poster), a dataset and benchmark for paper-to-poster generation as a visual coding task. RAP contains 25K+ verified paper-poster pairs from various machine learning conferences held between 2022 and 2026, annotated with a three-level content taxonomy, plus a held-out benchmark of 150 test papers each paired with its human poster. A fully automated derendering pipeline turns each poster into styled HTML/CSS of typed, refillable slots, so every sample is a paper-poster-markup triplet verifiable by rendering against the source poster. The corpus serves both inference and training: PosterRAG, a training-free multi-agent pipeline, retrieves an exemplar and refills its slots without touching layout or styling, while an SFT+RL recipe trains a dedicated model from the same triplets. An online demo is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[A100: A Hybrid Vision Foundation Model for Agricultural Plant Image Analysis](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22991)**  `arXiv:2610.22991`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mayur Pratim Das, Ajai Dubey, Aksshay Mathew P, Sana Afreen, Rishi Kumar Soni, Deepak Meena, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The use of accurate vision models for crop monitoring, disease diagnosis, and plant phenotyping in agriculture is hindered by the lack of expertly annotated data, especially in developing countries. We propose A100, an agricultural vision foundation model that is pre-trained using self-supervised learning on 3 million unlabeled images from fields covering more than 100 crop species. A100 uses a hybrid CNN-Transformer backbone that fuses MBConv stages for local feature extraction with Transformer stages for global contextual representation. A100 uses pre-training with the integration of DINO self-distillation, iBOT masked patch reconstruction, and KoLeo uniformity regularization, resulting in features that are better than ImageNet-12K supervised representations for agricultural applications without any fine-tuning. By utilizing a frozen backbone, the accuracy for crop classification of A100 is 95.37%, which is +2.56% better than ImageNet-12K, and reduces the leaf count error by 10.4% on the GroMo25 phenotyping benchmark, proving that the same backbone can be used for classification and regression tasks. After full fine-tuning, the performance of A100 is 99.90% on 32-class crop identification and 99.02% on 44-class disease detection. On the difficult PlantDoc dataset, A100 outperforms all baselines, such as CvT-13 (64.89% against 63.56%). Remarkably, A100 obtains 98.01% accuracy on plant classification using just 1% of the labelled data (about 284 images per category), showing great label efficiency for use cases with limited labeling. Under an identical protocol, A100 also outperforms the publicly released agricultural foundation models FoMo4Wheat and LeafVision by more than 14 points at 1% labels. We plan to publicly release our pretrained model to allow agricultural professionals to adapt A100 for various plant analysis applications using limited annotations.
  </details>

- **[ANCHOR: Audio-Visually Grounded Chain-of-Thought Reasoning Benchmark](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22964)**  `arXiv:2610.22964`  `cs.CV`  
  _Joel Julin, Souraja Kundu, Liza Dahiya, George Z. Wei, Liuyue Xie, Jingru Yi, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) perform well on audio-visual question answering, but correct answers do not necessarily imply that their stated reasoning is grounded in relevant perceptual evidence. Existing benchmarks mainly evaluate final-answer accuracy, offering limited insight into whether model-generated rationales correctly identify visual regions, temporal intervals, and audio events supporting predictions. We introduce ANCHOR, a benchmark and evaluation framework for measuring grounded-rationale alignment in multimodal reasoning, comprising 2,319 videos and 8,272 question-answer pairs with structured reasoning traces, temporal evidence, spatial bounding boxes, and salient audio cues, including a human-refined test set of 224 videos and 975 question-answer pairs. We separately measure answer correctness, hallucination, logical divergence, and conciseness, with direct spatial and temporal grounding measurements on a focused subset. Across the full benchmark, twelve contemporary MLLMs show a substantial alignment gap: models with comparable answer performance can differ markedly in whether their explanations are supported by annotated evidence. On a matched subset of 100 videos and 446 question-answer pairs, rankings remain highly stable across human-refined, Gemini-generated, and GPT-generated references (mean Pearson r=0.99), providing evidence against model-reference circularity within this subset. Grounding-cue ablations of three MLLMs on the full human-refined test set reveal a consistent modality hierarchy: removing video reduces accuracy by 38.4-41.8 points, while removing bounding boxes (3.2-7.7 points) and audio (4.2-5.6 points) causes smaller, complementary, model-dependent drops. These findings show answer-only evaluation obscures important grounding failures and position ANCHOR as a testbed for evaluating accurate and evidence-aligned audio-visual reasoning.
  </details>

- **[ODSS-Net: Orthogonally Decoupled Shared-Specific Representations for Multi-Modal Object Re-Identification](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22922)**  `arXiv:2610.22922`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhifeng Ma, Yuhong Feng, Chongyi Huang, Mingyang Zhou, Minwen Deng_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-modal object re-identification (ReID) leverages complementary information from heterogeneous sensors. Cross-modal alignment and fusion remain challenging. Existing methods mainly reduce modality gaps via feature alignment and fusion. However, alignment may entangle modality-shared structural information with modality-specific cues, while conventional fusion may not fully exploit complementary information. To address these issues, we propose ODSS-Net, a framework for orthogonally decoupled shared-specific representation learning and adaptive fusion of complementary modality-specific features. First, a Shared Structural Feature Construction (SSFC) module suppresses modality-dependent appearance variations and extracts modality-shared structural information. Then, an Orthogonal Specific Feature Learning (OSFL) module aggregates identity-relevant local information and removes the shared-aligned component via geometric orthogonal projection. Finally, a Sample Adaptive Mixture of Experts (SAMoE) module uses the shared structure as routing context to adaptively refine and fuse modality-specific features. Experiments on three benchmarks demonstrate the effectiveness of ODSS-Net for multi-modal object ReID.
  </details>

- **[VTrajEmbed: Aligning Text with Video Object Trajectories via Spatiotemporal Embeddings](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22842)**  `arXiv:2610.22842`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ruizhi Chen, Zining Fan, Yudi Guo, Jiang Liu, Zhiyu Lin, Zhongjiang He, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Fine-grained alignment between text and video object trajectories is a core challenge in multimodal understanding. Existing embedding models excel at global retrieval, yet their single global representation entangles multiple objects and their spatiotemporal dynamics, limiting direct object-level grounding. To address this issue, we propose VTrajEmbed, an embedding model that decomposes a video into object trajectories and aligns each trajectory embedding with text for object-level grounding through retrieval. Our main contributions: (1)Slow-Fast Trajectory Encoding, a dual-branch architecture that establishes trajectory representations by densely sampling low-resolution frames for high-frequency motion cues and sparsely selecting high-resolution frames for scene context, aggregated by a shared LLM backbone; (2)Mask-Guided Contrastive Learning, a training objective built upon these representations that couples an intra-group loss and a batch-level bidirectional loss under a relation mask, excluding unverified relations and preventing confirmed matches from becoming false negatives; (3)Text2Traj-Bench, a trajectory-level text-video benchmark with model-assisted construction and human-verified query--trajectory relations. Experimental results show that VTrajEmbed-5B improves text-to-trajectory and trajectory-to-text R@1 over Qwen3-VL-Embedding-8B by 15.48 and 18.05 percentage points, respectively, and achieves 49.48% average R@1 across five global retrieval benchmarks, compared with 42.86% for Qwen3-VL-Embedding-8B.
  </details>

- **[VISTA: A Multimodal Egocentric Benchmark for Goal-Oriented Perception and Assistance to Blind and Low-Vision Users](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22822)**  `arXiv:2610.22822`  `cs.CV`  
  _Noé Mosca, Mohamed Rayan Barhdadi, Muhammad Hamza, Joshua Yeh, Suryodaya Bikram Shahi, Zhiyan Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Blindness and low vision limit many people's ability to complete daily tasks, motivating AI assistants that provide useful guidance. Yet, existing benchmarks do not adequately test whether vision models can provide effective everyday assistance. In particular, i) existing benchmarks primarily rely on visual and textual inputs, with limited support for other modalities that are increasingly available in egocentric wearable devices such as gaze, inertial measurements, spatial trajectories, and others; and ii) existing evaluations emphasize scene understanding and question answering, with limited coverage of goal-conditioned guidance for blind and low-vision users across everyday tasks. To bridge this gap, we introduce VISTA (Visually Impaired Scene and Task Annotations), a multimodal egocentric benchmark for goal-oriented assistance for blind and low-vision users. VISTA contains $\sim$1,000 egocentric recordings captured with Project Aria glasses; each recording provides synchronized sensor streams, six of which serve as model inputs, and three different types of annotation, covering ten task categories (TC), from object localization to hazard detection. We evaluate 14 representative vision-language models zero-shot on VISTA and design a lightweight multimodal model baseline that integrates all six modalities. Experimental results show that, although existing models can often describe egocentric scenes, they remain limited in generating goal-directed assistive guidance. VISTA thus provides a benchmark for evaluating multimodal models that could improve everyday accessibility.
  </details>

- **[Direct Conditional Parameterization for N-Dimensional Splatting](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22778)**  `arXiv:2610.22778`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhongpai Gao, Benjamin Planche, Van Nguyen Nguyen, Meng Zheng, Anwesa Choudhuri, Arun Innanje, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  N-dimensional splatting extends 3D Gaussian Splatting with view direction and time to model view-dependent and dynamic effects. Each primitive is lifted into a joint distribution over position and conditioning variables, then conditionally sliced at each rendering query. N-DGS and UBS share this step across Gaussian and Beta kernels: recovering the conditional mean, opacity, and covariance requires per-primitive matrix inversions and regression products in every forward pass. We introduce direct conditional parameterization, replacing this covariance-derived readout with explicit per-effect parameters: a Cholesky precision factor for opacity and a factorized query-to-position regression operator with learnable per-dimension coupling. Unrestricted conditional coordinates recover a valid joint covariance; our factorization adds spatial-scale and coupling priors. Applied to both kernels, the parameterization yields dGS (Gaussian) and dBS (Beta). Across five static and dynamic benchmarks, they match or exceed their covariance-derived counterparts on quality, with gains up to +1.26 dB in the main MCMC evaluation. Production slicing is 6.9-7.7 times faster than on-the-fly covariance-derived conditioning; complete-render profiling gives 1.25-1.91 times speedups on four trained scenes. Direct conditioning provides a structured, kernel-agnostic alternative for N-dimensional splatting. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[Input- and Feature-Space Correction for Cross-Hospital IHC Grading](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22756)**  `arXiv:2610.22756`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jongwoon Lee, Jhair Gallardo, Christopher Kanan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pathology foundation models (FMs) are trained predominantly on hematoxylin and eosin (H&E) images but are increasingly used as frozen feature extractors for immunohistochemistry (IHC), where cross-hospital differences can impair grading. However, systematic comparisons of correction methods for cross-hospital IHC grading remain limited. To address this gap, we benchmark label-free transformations applied to input images before frozen-FM encoding and to features after frozen-FM encoding using UNI and Virchow2, three IHC markers, and six directed cross-hospital transfers, yielding 12 settings. Among the evaluated input-space methods, our proposed method FIX achieved the largest macro-F1 improvement over no correction, outperforming well-known stain normalization techniques. Among feature-space methods, a simple feature-wise standardization achieved the best improvement over no correction, outperforming CORAL. Under patient-averaged tile-level evaluation, combining FIX with feature-wise standardization improved macro-F1 and quadratic weighted kappa (QWK) by 0.181 and 0.208, respectively, and reduced ordinal error by 0.209 relative to no correction. These results show that input- and feature-space transformations can provide complementary benefits for cross-hospital IHC grading with frozen pathology FMs.
  </details>

- **[MedSTP: Preserving Subspace and Transition Evidence for Medical VQA Token Pruning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22751)**  `arXiv:2610.22751`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mengchen Fan, Qizhen Lan, Xi Xiao, Lin Zhao, Liang He, Tianyang Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Medical vision-language models (VLMs) for visual question answering (VQA) often pass long visual-token sequences to the language model, making visual-token pruning attractive but risky for medical images, where answers may depend on localized boundary and texture evidence. We study training free pre-decoding pruning and treat pruning as a subspace stable evidence preservation problem. The retained tokens should remain close to the dominant visual evidence subspace induced by the full token set. To this end, we propose \emph{Subspace-Transition Token Pruning} (\method{}), which estimates each token's spectral support from principal component space and constructs a compact representation subspace. To better reflect medical image structure, \method{} further uses a bounded token-grid transition prior to promote boundary and texture sensitive tokens. We evaluate \method{} on the closed-ended SLAKE and OmniMedVQA benchmarks and on RadImageNet-VQA, which provides both closed-ended and open-ended evaluations, using Lingshu-7B and Qwen3-VL-8B backbones. \method{} delivers consistently strong performance on both the closed-ended and open-ended RadImageNet-VQA evaluations. These results show that \method{} is an effective training free pruning framework for medical domain VQA.
  </details>

- **[OmniXRef: A Unified Framework for Multimodal Multi-Reference Video Editing](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22744)**  `arXiv:2610.22744`  `cs.CV`  
  _Xiaoyan Cong, Tianyu Wang, Hyeonho Jeong, Zhifei Zhang, Yuqian Zhou, Yijun Li, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Creative intent in video editing can be expressed through text instructions (e.g., "remove the people"), image references (e.g., subject appearance), video references (e.g., camera movement), or a combination of the above. We introduce OmniXRef, a unified framework for multimodal multi-reference video editing that allows users to combine variable numbers of text, image, and video references to control diverse attributes of a source video. We first devise a unified input design with a reference-bank interface and introduce $R^2$-RoPE to support generalization to more references. Then our model, OmniXRef-Model, uses a single-stream diffusion transformer to jointly process the source video and multimodal context from an MLLM in an interleaved sequence. To address the scarcity of training data for video-reference editing, we construct OmniXRef-Data using synthesis pipelines for different editing tasks and combinations of references. We use curriculum learning to progressively increase task complexity and resolution. To support systematic evaluation of video as a control modality, we introduce OmniXRef-Bench, a benchmark for video-reference attribute transfer across Camera, Motion, Content, Style, and Visual Effects (VFX). Comprehensive evaluations show that our OmniXRef-Model outperforms task-specific baselines and commercial models.
  </details>

- **[Thinking from Above: Efficient Spatial Reasoning with Bird's-Eye-View Visual Tokens](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22700)**  `arXiv:2610.22700`  `cs.CV`  
  _Faizan Siddiqui, Nirav Savaliya, Sahith Reddy Chada, Enna Sachdeva_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Existing video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) organize observations primarily in time, but embodied spatial reasoning requires organizing them in space. Consequently, their visual representations can remain locally consistent across nearby frames while lacking a persistent spatial context that is stable across viewpoints and time. We exploit depth and camera pose, which are naturally available to embodied agents, to explicitly establish this spatial correspondence. We introduce Cambrian-BEV, which extends Cambrian-S with a parameter-free geometric tokenization scheme that back-projects visual patch features from each frame into a shared bird's-eye-view (BEV) coordinate frame and averages features that fall within the same spatial cell. Thus, observations of the same physical location, regardless of viewpoint or time step, are aggregated into a common spatial token. In addition to providing a persistent scene representation, this formulation acts as an efficient compression mechanism: 128 frames containing approximately 100K DINOv3 patch features are consolidated into a fixed 27$\times$27 grid of only 729 BEV tokens, cutting LLM prefill time by $2.9\times$ and peak memory by $3.6\times$. Cambrian-BEV requires no architectural modification beyond BEV tokenization and uses a frozen DINOv3 vision encoder with a trainable multimodal projector and LLM. On VSI-Bench, our model reaches 63.48, $+8.5$ points over an identically fine-tuned frame-token control with $12.6\times$ fewer visual tokens, with particularly large gains on room size ($+24.5$) and relative direction ($+34.1$). On ReVSI, it reaches 44.68, $+4.5$ over the corresponding control.
  </details>

- **[EgoP$^{2}$C: Internalizing Plan-Perceive-Compose for Spatial Reasoning in Egocentric Video](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22681)**  `arXiv:2610.22681`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chaoyu Li, Arka Sadhu, Yuliang Li, Hyo Jin Kim, Yuning Chai, Pooyan Fazli_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Answering complex spatial reasoning questions from egocentric video requires determining what evidence is needed, acquiring targeted observations, and combining them through multiple reasoning steps. Existing vision-language models (VLMs) struggle to perform this process reliably. Agentic systems address this challenge by querying external vision and geometry tools, but training their reasoning leaves these tools unchanged, so inaccurate observations can still lead to incorrect answers. We introduce EgoP$^{2}$C, a framework that internalizes a plan-perceive-compose process within a single VLM. To this end, we first train specialized Planner and Perception teachers using separate adapters (Dual-LoRA), then fine-tune the corresponding experts in a mixture-of-experts (MoE) student under their supervision. We subsequently train the student on trajectories generated by its current policy using a segment-level extension of multi-teacher on-policy distillation (MOPD), assigning different trajectory segments to different supervisors. The Planner teacher supervises which evidence-seeking questions to ask, when to stop gathering evidence, and how to compose the final answer. The Perception teacher supervises the video-grounded answers to those questions. At inference, the student executes this adaptive workflow end-to-end, without external spatial tools. Across three spatial reasoning benchmarks, EgoP$^{2}$C students built from Qwen3-VL-8B and SenseNova-SI-1.3-8B outperform their base models by an average of 9.2 and 7.5 percentage points, respectively. Across both backbones, EgoP$^{2}$C reduces inference FLOPs by 65.8-66.3% and achieves $1.29$-$1.49\times$ speedups over their shared Dual-LoRA teacher.
  </details>

- **[Stuck on Shuffle: On Attribute-Object Binding in Vision-Language Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22602)**  `arXiv:2610.22602`  `cs.CV`  
  _Netanel Y. Tamir, Michael Green, Issar Tzachor, Or Shimshi, Dvir Samuel, Or Patashnik, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Retrieval is a fundamental task in multimodal systems: given an image or text query, the goal is to find the most relevant images or texts in a collection, often using a shared embedding space. Reliable retrieval requires these embeddings to capture not only which concepts are present, but also how they are composed. One key compositional challenge is attribute--object binding: encoding which attributes belong to which objects. Existing benchmarks provide a limited view of this capability, typically covering a subset of modality pairings, simple two-object swaps, or small attribute sets. Consequently, it remains unclear how robustly current models preserve attribute--object bindings across different modality pairings and more complex settings. We therefore introduce SABER, a controlled benchmark for evaluating attribute--object binding across all four image and text modality pairings, spanning diverse attribute types and scenes with up to ten objects. Evaluating models on SABER reveals substantial binding failures in both CLIP-style dual encoders and recent MLLM-based embedders. These findings motivate us to ask how binding can be improved efficiently. While multimodal supervision may be effective, collecting suitable images is difficult and costly. Text, on the other hand, is well suited to binding supervision: it is cheap to generate, supports a broader range of attributes and enables precisely controlled hard negatives. We thus finetune an MLLM-based embedder exclusively on synthetic attribute-binding text. Despite the absence of image supervision, the gains transfer to cross-modal and image--image retrieval while preserving general multimodal embedding performance. Our model achieves SOTA results on SABER while also improving on negation and instance-retrieval benchmarks, demonstrating broader benefits of our binding-focused supervision.
  </details>

- **[Event-STVG: Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding for Long-Form Event Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22444)**  `arXiv:2610.22444`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yizhe Zhang, Xujia Wang, Songqi Tang, Xujie Tang, Leo Jiahao Jiang, Jiacheng Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spatio-Temporal Video Grounding (STVG) aims to jointly localize a target in space and time given a natural-language query. Despite continued advances, existing STVG benchmarks remain largely centered on localizing targets through instance-specific descriptions, leaving them largely decoupled from long-form reasoning increasingly emphasized in multimodal large language models (MLLMs). In this paper, we introduce Event-STVG, a task that shifts STVG from locating what is described toward event-level reasoning by unifying long-form context, repeated-event reasoning, event localization, role inference, and multi-target grounding. To facilitate research on Event-STVG, we construct a large-scale benchmark comprising 4,016 annotated event occurrences across soccer and seven additional domains. Experiments with state-of-the-art MLLMs reveal substantial difficulty, with the strongest model achieving only 5.4% m-vIoU at the 10-minute horizon. Across domains, we consistently observe confusion among repeated same-type events, spatial grounding bottlenecks, and rare success in jointly grounding all required roles. Event-STVG establishes a new challenge for visual understanding in MLLMs, calling for future models that can jointly reason about complex events across space and time. Code and annotations are available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[WaveLLM: Wavelet-Domain LLM Entropy Modeling for Lossless Image Compression](https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.22232)**  `arXiv:2610.22232`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yue Yu, Bowei Kang, Changsheng Gao, Dong Liu, Xiaoyan Sun_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Lossless image compression fundamentally relies on accurate entropy modeling, driving a recent shift toward large language model (LLM)-based methods. However, existing approaches mainly emphasize the modeling capacity of LLMs while directly serializing images into pixel or residual sequences, leaving heterogeneous signal patterns entangled within a single prediction stream. In this paper, we argue that effective entropy modeling depends not only on model capacity, but also on the predictability of the input representation. Motivated by this insight, we propose WaveLLM, a wavelet-domain LLM-based entropy model for lossless image compression. By decomposing an image into multi-level wavelet subbands, WaveLLM converts a heterogeneous prediction problem into a set of more structured and statistically homogeneous subproblems that are more amenable to autoregressive probability estimation.
